## Domain 2: GPU PTX/CUDA Kernels, Roofline Simulator, QAT & Sparse Attention

> [!CAUTION]
> **[P0-4] BFloat16 Mantissa Overflow on Ternary Zero (`q = 1`) in `0xEA` Unpacker (FIXED)**
> **Locations:** [`w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu`](file:///d:/Coding_Projects/.Unified_Hardware_Accel/fused_w1_hadacore/w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu#L82-L103), [`ptx_bf162_emu.c`](file:///d:/Coding_Projects/.Unified_Hardware_Accel/sim_harness/ptx_bf162_emu.c#L150-L169), [`ptx_bf162_emu.c`](file:///d:/Coding_Projects/.Unified_Hardware_Accel/sim_harness/ptx_bf162_emu.c#L312-L355), [`spinquant_packer.py`](file:///d:/Coding_Projects/.Unified_Hardware_Accel/fused_w1_hadacore/spinquant_packer.py#L190-L219)
>
> **IEEE-754 BFloat16 Principle & Remediation:**
> Because $129 = 2^7 + 2^0$ spans all 8 significand bits of a BFloat16 number, pre-multiplying `bf16(-129.0f * alpha_g)` rounded off the lowest mantissa bits of any non-power-of-2 scale `alpha_g`, causing ternary zero (`q = 1`, encoded as `0x4001` = $2.0 + 1/64$) to unpack to `-0.25f` instead of `0.0f` for `alpha_g = 1.75f`.
> Replaced the pre-multiplied FMA constants with exact unscaled BF162 constants `c_mul = 0x42804280` `(64.0, 64.0)` and `c_sub = 0xC301C301` `(-129.0, -129.0)` in `fma.rn.bf16x2` to yield exact unit trits `{-1.0, +0.0, +1.0}` (`0xBF80`, `0x0000`, `0x3F80`), followed by `fma.rn.bf16x2` with `scale_bf162` and `-0.0` (`0x80008000`, `F1`). Verified across 18,874,368 ternary states in `ptx_bf162_emu.c` against the true mathematical target `{-alpha_g, 0.0f, +alpha_g}` with **0 bit errors**.

---

> [!CAUTION]
> **[P0-5] 2x Energy Underestimation & Half-Tile Omission in `tiled_cbc_sieve.cu` (FIXED)**
> **Locations:** [`tiled_cbc_sieve.cu`](file:///d:/Coding_Projects/.Unified_Hardware_Accel/structural_filters/tiled_cbc_sieve.cu#L31-L110)
>
> **SM86/SM89 Fragment Principle & Remediation:**
> Expanded `a_rot_k128` in `tiled_cbc_sieve_pred` from `[4][4][4]` (`64` `bf162` regs/lane = `K=64`) to the complete `[4][2][4][4]` (`4` steps of `K=32` $\times$ `2` `k16` sub-steps $\times$ `4` `m16` row tiles $\times$ `4` `bf162` regs = `128` `bf162` regs/lane = `8,192` BF16 elements across the 32-lane warp), and added a standalone host verification suite (`test_tiled_cbc.exe`) confirming exact energy normalization (`E_k = 1.5000` on `(-1.5, +1.5)` BF162 inputs).

---

> [!WARNING]
> **[P1-3] Shared-Memory `cp.async` + `ldmatrix` Staging, Hoisted Hadamard Signs & Boundary Guards in `w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu` (FIXED)**
> **Locations:** [`w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu`](file:///d:/Coding_Projects/.Unified_Hardware_Accel/fused_w1_hadacore/w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu#L125-L185), [`w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu`](file:///d:/Coding_Projects/.Unified_Hardware_Accel/fused_w1_hadacore/w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu#L270-L338)
>
> **SM86/SM89 Principle & Remediation:**
> Added `precompute_hadacore_h16_b_frag` to hoist the loop-invariant Sylvester Hadamard `H_16` B-fragment signs outside the `k_base` loop, wired up 128-byte XOR-swizzled shared-memory staging (`cp_async_16B` + `swizzle_128b_smem_offset` + `ldmatrix_x4_bf16`) for in-bounds tiles, and fixed the tail boundary guards to check `(k_sub + 2 * t + 1) < K` for `raw_a[0..1]` and `(k_sub + 2 * t + 9) < K` for `raw_a[2..3]`.

---

> [!WARNING]
> **[P1-4] `galore_async_offload.py` Root-Second-Moment ($\sqrt{v_t}$) Quantization & Device-Side INT8 Packing (FIXED)**
> **Locations:** [`galore_async_offload.py`](file:///d:/Coding_Projects/.Unified_Hardware_Accel/qat_galore_engine/galore_async_offload.py#L126-L172)
>
> **Numerical Principle & Remediation:**
> Stored and quantized the **root second moment** $\sqrt{v_t}$ in `uint8` (`exp_avg_sq_q`, `exp_avg_sq_scale`) instead of the raw squared moment $v_t$, matching the linear dynamic range of $m_t$ and preventing coordinates with $|R_{r,j}| < 0.044 \max_k |R_{r,k}|$ from underflowing to `0` and triggering $10^5\times$ Adam step spikes. Also quantized tensors to `int8`/`uint8` prior to `.cpu()` transfer so only 1-byte tensors cross PCIe.

---

> [!WARNING]
> **[P1-5] `lserve_sparse_attn.py` Per-Query Convex Upper Bound & Packed `uint8` INT4 Dequantizer (FIXED)**
> **Locations:** [`lserve_sparse_attn.py`](file:///d:/Coding_Projects/.Unified_Hardware_Accel/structural_filters/lserve_sparse_attn.py#L90-L178)
>
> **Mathematical Principle & Remediation:**
> Replaced `q_mean = q_rot.mean(dim=2)` before `torch.maximum` with per-query convex upper-bound evaluation `torch.maximum(q_exp * k_min_exp, q_exp * k_max_exp).sum(dim=-1).amax(dim=2)` so opposing-sign queries across `Sq` never cancel out, and added `unpack_kv_pages_u8_to_int8` to dequantize active KV pages directly from the 4,096-byte `k_packed_u8` and `v_packed_u8` buffers (achieving `0.99313` cosine similarity against dense FP32 attention).

## Domain 3: CPU ISA (AMX/AVX2/AVX-512), OS Virtual Memory & C++20 Smart Cache

> [!CAUTION]

> **Locations:** [`chorus_amx_moe.c`](file:///d:/Coding_Projects/.Unified_Hardware_Accel/hetero_runtime/chorus_amx_moe.c#L29-L48), [`chorus_amx_moe.c`](file:///d:/Coding_Projects/.Unified_Hardware_Accel/hetero_runtime/chorus_amx_moe.c#L75-L248)

> **Intel AMX ISA Principle & Remediation:**
> Updated `amx_gemm_1bit_expert_tile` to pack `w_vnni_buf` in hardware VNNI pair-interleaved order (`[K/2 = 16, N = 16, 2]`) via vectorized `_mm512_i32scatter_epi32` (AVX-512 path) and strict-aliasing-clean 32-bit VNNI pair packing (emulation path with AMX BF16/FP32 denormal flush-to-zero semantics, `O10`), padded `gpu_ready_seq` (offset `0`) and `cpu_done_seq` (offset `64`) onto separate 64-byte cache lines in `HeteroMoERingSlot`, added `VirtualLock`/`mlock` physical page-locking and a dedicated OS worker thread (`_beginthreadex`/`pthread_create`), and verified both uniform/non-uniform tiles and concurrent SPSC ring execution with **0 errors**.

> [!WARNING]
> **[P1-6] Unified `MemorySandbox` Bank Swizzles & `SmartTieredCache` W2 Execution + LFU/LRU Eviction (FIXED)**
> **Locations:** [`hardware_and_sandbox.hpp`](file:///d:/Coding_Projects/.Unified_Hardware_Accel/kinesis_unified/hardware_and_sandbox.hpp#L292-L355), [`sub2bit_gemm_and_smart_cache.hpp`](file:///d:/Coding_Projects/.Unified_Hardware_Accel/kinesis_unified/sub2bit_gemm_and_smart_cache.hpp#L370-L745), [`test_kinesis_unified.cpp`](file:///d:/Coding_Projects/.Unified_Hardware_Accel/kinesis_unified/test_kinesis_unified.cpp#L1-L260)
>
> **Architectural Principle & Remediation:**

> 2. Updated `pack_w2_gotoblas_panels` to compute the conditional L2-optimal scale over active non-zero trits, added LFU+LRU Tier-1 VRAM eviction (`record_panel_access_and_promote`), reordered the inner micro-kernel loop to `p -> ir -> g -> k` so `acc0..3` stay in YMM registers across all K-groups before writing `C`, and added `execute_smart_w2_gemm` (verified with `0.00e+00` error vs reference).

> **STATUS NOTICE:** The "Wave 2 Integration" and "Upgrades Applied" statements below describe code that is implemented and verified on the host CPU across 18 automated test suites (`python run_all_tests.py`), including host 256-fiber CTA / 32-lane Warp-Collective execution of the `.cu` kernels (`w1a16_host_cta_shim.hpp`). Because this environment has no physical NVIDIA GPU or `nvcc`, hardware `ptxas` compilation, live kernel-driver patching, and physical GPU throughput benchmarks remain for target hardware, and speedups quoted for our code are modelled rather than measured on silicon. See [`docs/11_Adversarial_Review_Status_and_Known_Limitations.md`](file:///d:/Coding_Projects/.Unified_Hardware_Accel/docs/11_Adversarial_Review_Status_and_Known_Limitations.md) for the complete ledger.

# 10. Related Papers, Open-Source Repositories, Community Benchmarks & Wave 2 Upgrades

This document synthesizes external academic literature, open-source repositories, official hardware documentation, and community engineering reports (`r/LocalLLaMA`, Hacker News, GPU MODE, Level1Techs, TechPowerUp) across our three core pillars:
1. **Sub-2-Bit (1-Bit Binary & 1.58-Bit Ternary) GPU PTX & CPU AVX2/AMX Kernels**
2. **Consumer GPU Hardware/Driver Unlocking, P2P BAR1 Drivers & CUDA Interception Shims**
3. **Heterogeneous CPU+GPU Inference, Smart Tiered Caching, Sparse KV-Cache & Low-Rank QAT**

It also documents how `.Unified_Hardware_Accel` compares to these projects and details the architectural upgrades extracted from them and integrated into our codebase in Wave 2.

---

## 1. Pillar 1: Sub-2-Bit GPU PTX & CPU AVX2/AVX-512 Kernels

### 1.1 Academic Papers, Repositories & Community Buzz

| Project / Framework | Repository & Primary Paper | Core Hardware / Algorithmic Mechanism | Community Benchmarks (`r/LocalLLaMA`, HN, GPU MODE) |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **BitNet (`bitnet.cpp`)** | `https://github.com/microsoft/BitNet`<br>`arXiv:2402.17764` (*The Era of 1-bit LLMs*)<br>`arXiv:2502.11880` (*BitNet.cpp*) | Three CPU kernel families: **`I2_S`** (2-bit weight unpack + `_mm256_maddubs_epi16`), **`TL1`** (2-trit / 9-entry LUT in two `vpshufb` tables), and **`TL2`** (3-trit / 27-state sign-mirror 14-entry LUT + `vpsignb` at 1.67 bpw). | Major traction on `r/LocalLLaMA` and Hacker News with `BitNet-b1.58-2B-4T`: **2.37x to 6.17x speedup** and **71.9% to 82.2% energy reduction** on x86 CPUs; runs a 100B ternary model on a single CPU at 5 to 7 tok/s. |
| **T-MAC** | `https://github.com/microsoft/T-MAC`<br>EuroSys 2025 (`arXiv:2407.00088`) | Replaces per-weight FMA unpacking with **bit-serial Lookup Tables (LUTs)** of size $2^g = 16$ ($g = 4$) broadcast into both 128-bit lanes of an AVX2 `YMM` register (`_mm256_broadcastsi128_si256`) and queried in 1 cycle via `_mm256_shuffle_epi8` (`vpshufb`). | Outperforms `llama.cpp` `Q4_0`/`Q2_K` by **3x to 4x on CPU** (71 tok/s on M2 Ultra, 11 tok/s on Raspberry Pi 5 for 3B 1.58-bit, with linear throughput scaling from 4-bit down to 1-bit). |
| **Marlin** | `https://github.com/IST-DASLab/marlin`<br>`arXiv:2408.11743` (*MARLIN*) | 4-stage `cp.async.ca`/`.cg` software pipeline + double-buffered `ldmatrix.sync.aligned.m8n8.x4` + register `lop3.b32` + **Striped Stream-K** CTAs across all SMs with L2-resident reductions. | Standard low-bit GPU kernel in `vLLM` and `GPU MODE`: sustains **>90% of peak DRAM bandwidth** up to batch size $M = 16..32$ (**3.87x speedup** over FP16 cuBLAS on RTX 4090 / A100). |
| **BitBLAS / Ladder** | `https://github.com/microsoft/BitBLAS`<br>OSDI 2024 (`arXiv:2312.06097`) | TileLang/TVM micro-kernel generator supporting `W1A16`, `W2A16` (ternary), and `W1.58A8` via hardware-aligned layout propagation (`mma.m16n8k16`) and branchless `lop3.b32` register bit-slicing. | Official GPU backend for `microsoft/BitNet` (`gpu/` directory) and `vLLM` BitNet integration; delivers **2.5x to 3.9x** decode speedup on RTX 4090 for W2A16 ternary models. |
| **HadaCore** | `https://github.com/HandH1998/HadaCore`<br>`arXiv:2412.08832` (*HadaCore*) | Reformulates Fast Walsh-Hadamard Transform (FWHT) from CUDA-core radix-2 butterflies into **radix-16 Tensor Core `mma.sync.aligned.m16n8k16`** steps + Kronecker shared-memory transpose for $K = 256..4096$. | Delivers up to **3.6x speedup** over CUDA-core butterfly FWHT kernels and reduces online rotation overhead in `SpinQuant`/`QuaRot` to `<2%` of total runtime. |
| **SpinQuant** | `https://github.com/facebookresearch/SpinQuant`<br>ICLR 2025 (`arXiv:2405.16406`) | Optimizes orthogonal rotation matrices $R_1, R_2 \in \mathcal{O}(d)$ on the **Stiefel manifold via Cayley SGD**; absorbs $R_1, R_2$ offline into weights and applies online Kronecker Hadamard rotations $R_3, R_4$. | Closes the accuracy gap of W4A4KV4 and sub-2-bit models to within **0.1 to 0.3 perplexity** of FP16 without dense runtime matrix multiplications. |
| **QuIP#** | `https://github.com/Cornell-RelaxML/quip-sharp`<br>ICML 2024 (`arXiv:2402.04396`) | Replaces dense rotations with **Randomized Kronecker-Hadamard Transforms** ($H_K = (H_p \otimes H_q) \cdot \text{diag}(s)$ where $s_i \in \{\pm 1\}$) so large $K = p \cdot q$ dimensions rotate in $O(K(p+q))$ FLOPs. | Widely used in `r/LocalLLaMA` for 2-bit Llama-2/3 models that outperform 3-bit GGUF on perplexity while decoding at **>150 tok/s** on RTX 4090. |
| **FLUTE** | `https://github.com/HanGuo97/flute`<br>EMNLP 2024 (`arXiv:2407.10960`) | Accelerates LUT-quantized 2-bit/3-bit/4-bit GEMM on GPUs using register `prmt.b32` + `lop3.b32` and banked shared-memory `ld.shared.v2.b32` LUT duplication + Stream-K scheduling. | Solves shared-memory bank conflicts in GPU LUT dequantization, achieving **2x to 3x speedup** over naive LUT kernels at $M \le 32$. |

### 1.2 Technical Synthesis & Extracted Wave 2 Upgrades

1. **T-MAC / `bitnet.cpp` AVX2 `_mm256_shuffle_epi8` (`vpshufb`) 16-Entry LUT Micro-Kernel vs. 4x8 AVX2 FMA Unpacker:**
   * For a group of $g = 4$ binary weights $(b_0, b_1, b_2, b_3) \in \{0, 1\}^4$ packed into a 4-bit nibble $idx = \sum_{j=0}^3 b_j 2^j \in \{0, \dots, 15\}$, any 4 activations $(a_0, a_1, a_2, a_3)$ have only 16 possible signed partial sums:
     $$T[idx] = \sum_{j=0}^{3} (2 b_j - 1) \cdot a_j, \quad idx \in \{0, \dots, 15\}$$
   * By quantizing the 16 partial sums to `int16_t` and splitting them into a 16-byte low-byte table `lut_lo` and a 16-byte high-byte table `lut_hi` broadcast into both 128-bit lanes of two AVX2 `YMM` registers via `_mm256_broadcastsi128_si256`, two `_mm256_shuffle_epi8` (`vpshufb`) instructions evaluate **32 4-weight dot products (128 binary MACs)** in parallel!
   * **Wave 2 Integration (`kinesis_unified/sub2bit_gemm_and_smart_cache.hpp`):** We added `W1TmacLutPanelSet`, `pack_w1_tmac_lut_panels`, and `execute_tmac_lut_w1_gemm` alongside our exact FP32 `4x8` FMA unpacker (`execute_smart_w1_gemm`), optionally gated by the Tiled-CBC activation energy sieve. It hoists `lut_lo_256[32]` and `lut_hi_256[32]` (`_mm256_broadcastsi128_si256`) outside the panel loop and processes 4 panels (`32` columns) simultaneously via 256-bit 32-lane `_mm256_shuffle_epi8` (`F14`, with a 1-panel 128-bit `_mm_shuffle_epi8` tail path), verified bit-identical (`0.00e+00`) against its scalar int8-LUT reference and within the int8 rounding bound of FP32.

2. **Marlin L2 Cache Persistence (`cp.async.ca` vs `cp.async.cg`), Ping-Pong Staging & Small-Batch Stream-K (`BM = 16`):**
   * During small-batch decode ($M \le 16$), activations $A$ ($[M, K]$) are reused across all $N$ columns while weights $W$ ($[N, K]$) stream once. Loading $A$ with `cp.async.ca.shared.global` (cache in L1 + L2) and streaming $W$ with `cp.async.cg.shared.global` prevents streaming weights from evicting $A$ out of L2.
   * Furthermore, when $M \le 16$ and $N = 4096$, a 2D CTA grid with `BM=128, BN=256` launches only $16$ CTAs, leaving $112$ of $128$ SMs idle on RTX 4090 ($87.5\%$ idle SMs!).
   * **Wave 2 Integration (`fused_w1_hadacore/w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu` & `sim_harness/smsp_roofline_sim.py`):** We implemented `cp_async_ca_16B`, double-buffered shared memory ping-pong staging (`smem_a_tile[2][128 * 128]`), `fused_w2a16_hadacore_gemm_kernel` (ternary 2-bit global kernel), and `marlin_streamk_w1a16_decode_kernel` (`BM=16` Split-K / Stream-K small-batch decode kernel with deterministic `marlin_streamk_reduce_slices_kernel` slice reduction `O16`), compiled and verified on the host via the 256-fiber CTA / 32-lane warp execution engine (`O1`), plus Stream-K wave-quantization modeling in `smsp_roofline_sim.py`.

3. **Kronecker-Hadamard ($H_p \otimes H_q$) Transform (`QuIP#`, `SpinQuant`, `HadaCore`):**
   * By the Kronecker mixed-product identity, for any activation vector $x \in \mathbb{R}^{p \cdot q}$ reshaped as $\text{Mat}_{p \times q}(x) \in \mathbb{R}^{p \times q}$:
     $$\text{Mat}_{p \times q}\big(x \cdot (H_p \otimes H_q)\big) = H_p^\top \cdot \text{Mat}_{p \times q}(x) \cdot H_q$$
   * For a $K = 32 = 2 \times 16$ warp step ($H_{32} = H_2 \otimes H_{16}$) or a $g = 128 = 8 \times 16$ quantization group ($H_{128} = H_8 \otimes H_{16}$), right-multiplication by $H_{16}$ executes on Tensor Cores via `hadacore_rotate_m16k16_in_regs`, and left-multiplication by $H_2$ or $H_8$ mixes the sub-tiles in registers via `fma.rn.bf16x2` butterflies!
   * **Wave 2 Integration (`w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu`, `ptx_bf162_emu.c`, `spinquant_packer.py`):** We added `hadacore_kronecker_h32_m16k32_in_regs` in `w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu`, full 2-stage $H_2 \otimes H_{16}$ + W2A16 ternary MMA verification in `ptx_bf162_emu.c` (`O12`, `F18`), and both `build_kernel_online_rotation` (exact fixed kernel rotation `F4`) and `optimize_kronecker_stiefel_rotation` (learned factorized Kronecker-Stiefel $R_{\text{kron}} = R_P \otimes H_{16}$ rotation `F13`, absorbed offline into $W_O' = (R_P^\top \otimes I_{16}) W_O$) in `spinquant_packer.py`.

## 2. Pillar 2: Consumer GPU Driver Unlocking, P2P BAR1 Patches & Interception Shims

### 2.1 Open-Source Projects, Official Docs & Community Reports

| Project / Source | URL & Maintainer | Technical Mechanism & Scope | Community Findings & Edge Cases |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **`tinygrad` & `aikitoria` P2P Open Kernel Modules** | `https://github.com/tinygrad/open-gpu-kernel-modules`<br>`https://github.com/aikitoria/open-gpu-kernel-modules` | Forces Hopper `kbusEnableStaticBar1Mapping_GH100` in `kern_bus_gm107.c`, overrides GeForce checks in `getP2PCaps`, sets `pMemDesc->pGpu = pRemoteGpu`, and changes `*pAperture = GMMU_APERTURE_SYS_NONCOH` in `nvGpuOpsBuildExternalAllocPtes`. | Tested across 550.54.15, 550.90.07, 560, 565, and 570+ drivers on RTX 3090, 4090, and 5090. Requires `iommu=pt` and ACS Request Redirection disabled (`ECAP_ACS+0x6.w=0000`). |
| **Modded 48GB RTX 4090 Reports (`r/LocalLLaMA`, TechPowerUp)** | Duanyll 48GB P2P Analysis & `r/LocalLLaMA` 48GB 4090 threads | Clamshell PCB swap with 24x 2GB GDDR6X modules. VBIOS or motherboard UEFI often caps `PCI_EXT_CAP_ID_REBAR` BAR1 at **32GB** ($32\text{ GB} < 48\text{ GB FB}$). | Stock `tinygrad` P2P fails on 48GB 4090s when `BAR1 = 32GB` because `bStaticBar1Enabled` requires `apertureLength >= fbLength + cpuVisibleLength`, causing `Xid 31` faults above 32GB without dynamic BAR1 LRU mapping. |
| **`vgpu_unlock` & `vgpu_unlock-rs`** | `https://github.com/DualCoder/vgpu_unlock`<br>`https://github.com/mbil123/vgpu_unlock-rs` | Intercepts `NV_ESC_RM_CONTROL` (`0xC020462A`) `ioctl` command `0x20801801` (`NV2080_CTRL_CMD_BUS_GET_PCI_INFO`) and kernel BAR0 reads (`0x00000000` `NV_PMC_BOOT_0`, `0x00088000` `NV_PCFG`). | On 550+ drivers with active GSP firmware (`gsp_ga10x.bin`), PCI ID validation moves inside the RISC-V GSP (`RMDisableFeatureDisablement=1`). |
| **`HAMi-core` & `ZLUDA` Driver Shims** | `https://github.com/Project-HAMi/HAMi-core`<br>`https://github.com/vosen/ZLUDA` | Intercepts `cuGetProcAddress` and `cuGetProcAddress_v2` in `libcuda.so` to replace internal CUDA 12.x driver jump tables. | CUDA 12.4+ calls `cuGetProcAddress_v2("cuGetProcAddress_v2", ...)` recursively to obtain an unhooked resolver and checks `*symbolStatus == CU_GET_PROC_ADDRESS_SUCCESS` (`0`). |
| **`NVIDIA/open-gpu-doc` & `envytools`** | `https://github.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-doc`<br>`https://github.com/envytools/envytools` | Official NVIDIA documentation on Falcon NS/LS/HS security modes (`Falcon-Security.txt`), `PRIV_LEVEL_MASK` (`PLM`), and `NV_PFUSE` (`0x00823000..0x00823FFF`). | Host BAR0 writes run at PL0 (NS) and are dropped (`0xBADF5040`) unless an HS Falcon payload (PL3) temporarily unlocks `NV_PFUSE_FEAT_OVR_PLM` (`0x00823804`) and re-locks it to `0x88000000`. |

### 2.2 Where `.Unified_Hardware_Accel` Leads & Wave 2 Upgrades

* **Existing Advantages in `.Unified_Hardware_Accel`:**
  1. Unlike `tinygrad/open-gpu-kernel-modules` (which only supports Static BAR1 and fails on 48GB modded RTX 4090s with 32GB BAR1 windows), `kernel_driver_patch/nv_gpu_ops_and_s3_unlock.c` implements a **dual-path Static + 8-Way LRU Dynamic BAR1 mapper** (`_nvGpuOpsDynBar1GetOrCreate`) plus **S3 Suspend/Resume Falcon speed-selector re-application** (`nv_pci_resume_early_unlock`) with `PRIV_LEVEL_MASK` re-locking (`0x88000000`).
  2. Unlike `vgpu_unlock-rs` (which globally spoofs PCI IDs and can confuse NCCL/PyTorch SM count queries), `compiler_unlock/libcublas_unlock_shim.c` uses `dladdr(__builtin_return_address(0))` to selectively spoof PCI Device IDs only for `libcublas` / `libcublasLt` / `libcudnn` while preserving true physical SM counts.
* **Wave 2 Upgrades Applied (`compiler_unlock/libcublas_unlock_shim.c` & `kernel_driver_patch/`):**
  1. Added **recursive `cuGetProcAddress` / `cuGetProcAddress_v2` self-interception** and explicit `*symbolStatus = 0` (`CU_GET_PROC_ADDRESS_SUCCESS`) population (from `ZLUDA` / `HAMi-core`).
  2. Added **`PCI_ID_RTX_4090_D` (`0x2685`)** and **`PCI_ID_RTX_5090` (`0x2B85` -> `0x2BB1` RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell)** mappings to `libcublas_unlock_shim.c`.
  3. Added upstream bridge **PCIe ACS Request/Completion Redirection clearing** (`PCI_ACS_RR | PCI_ACS_CR | PCI_ACS_UF`) as an explicit opt-in helper in the mock `nv_gpu_ops_and_s3_unlock.c` (never invoked automatically, because it weakens isolation).

## 3. Pillar 3: Heterogeneous CPU+GPU Inference, Smart Caching, Sparse KV & Q-GaLore

### 3.1 Academic Papers, Repositories & Community Benchmarks

| System / Method | Repository & Primary Citation | Key Hardware / Algorithmic Mechanism | Reported Benchmarks (`r/LocalLLaMA` & Papers) |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **KTransformers** | `https://github.com/kvcache-ai/ktransformers`<br>Tsinghua MADSys / Approaching.AI | Keeps dense MLA/GQA attention and KV cache in single-GPU VRAM while offloading routed MoE experts to CPU AMX/AVX-512/AVX2 via **CUDA Graph captured decode loops** and pinned host rings. | Runs **671B DeepSeek-R1/V3 (Q4_K_M)** on **1x 24GB RTX 4090 + DDR5 RAM** at **13.5 to 14.2 tok/s decode** and **286 tok/s prefill** (up to 27.8x faster prefill than `llama.cpp`). |
| **PowerInfer & PowerInfer-2** | `https://github.com/SJTU-IPADS/PowerInfer`<br>SOSP 2024 (`arXiv:2312.12456`)<br>`arXiv:2406.06282` | Exploits **power-law neuron activation locality** (10-20% hot neurons drive >80% of activation energy); pre-loads hot neuron clusters into GPU VRAM and computes active cold clusters on CPU with online frequency aging. | Achieves **13.20 tok/s avg (29.08 tok/s peak)** on 40B-175B models on a single RTX 4090 (**4.23x to 11.69x faster** than `llama.cpp`). |
| **Quest & LServe (OmniServe)** | `https://github.com/mit-han-lab/Quest`<br>`https://github.com/mit-han-lab/omniserve`<br>ICML '24 (`arXiv:2406.10774`), MLSys '25 (`arXiv:2502.14866`) | **Quest**: per-page channel $\min/\max$ key bounding boxes $\hat{s}_p(q) = \sum_d \max(q_d k_{p,\min}[d], q_d k_{p,\max}[d])$. **LServe**: splits heads into 50% static streaming heads (sink + local window only) and 50% dynamic Quest retrieval heads with INT4 Hadamard KV cache. | **Quest**: **7.03x self-attention speedup** (2.23x end-to-end). **LServe**: **2.9x prefill** and **1.3x to 2.1x decode speedup** at 128K-512K context lengths. |
| **GaLore & Q-GaLore** | `https://github.com/jiaweizzhao/GaLore`<br>`https://github.com/VITA-Group/Q-GaLore`<br>ICML '24 (`arXiv:2403.03507`), `arXiv:2407.08296` | **Q-GaLore**: quantizes projection matrices $P_t \in \mathbb{R}^{m \times r}$ to **INT4 with stochastic rounding** and uses **cosine-similarity adaptive lazy SVD updates** (doubling the SVD interval when subspace similarity $\ge 0.90$) + SVD-free power iteration. | Pre-trains LLaMA-7B from scratch on a **single 16GB RTX 4060 Ti** and cuts fine-tuning memory by **50%** below GaLore while eliminating **65% to 80% of SVD calls**. |

### 3.2 Extracted Wave 2 Upgrades Across Our Codebase

1. **Scale-Weighted Row-Maximum Holder Sub-Tile Sieve (`structural_filters/tiled_cbc_sieve.cu` & `tiled_cbc_sieve.py`):**
   * Combining Quest's upper-bound philosophy with PowerInfer's hot-tile weighting, we implemented `warp_reduce_rowmax_l1_k32`, `tiled_cbc_subtile_rowmax_bound_mask`, and `tiled_cbc_sieve_scale_weighted_sub32_mask`, which bound the worst-case output contribution of each $K=32$ sub-tile via the exact Holder $(L_1, L_\infty)$ inequality $\max_{m} \|A_{m, k32}\|_1 \cdot \alpha_{\max}$ (`O9`) and return a 4-bit mask across `BLOCK_K = 128` (executed and verified on the 32-lane warp-collective host engine).
2. **PowerInfer Offline Power-Law Calibration + Windowed Frequency Decay (`kinesis_unified/sub2bit_gemm_and_smart_cache.hpp`):**
   * Added `seed_offline_powerlaw_hot_panels` (pre-populating Tier-1 GPU VRAM at load time using offline calibration L1/frequency scores to eliminate cold-start compulsory misses) and `decay_access_frequencies` (windowed right-shift aging `count >>= 1` from PowerInfer-2 so topic shifts smoothly evict stale VRAM panels).
3. **LServe Hybrid Static Streaming Heads + Dynamic Quest Retrieval Heads (`structural_filters/lserve_sparse_attn.py`):**
   * Added `streaming_head_ratio` and `profile_streaming_head_mask` (`F15`) in `lserve_sparse_hadamard_attention`. Static streaming heads attend strictly to sink + local pages without computing dynamic bounding-box scores; selecting streaming heads via offline out-of-window vs. in-window centroid salience profiling (`profile_streaming_head_mask`) preserves **`0.99305` cosine similarity** at 50% streaming heads on the planted-needle test (whereas unprofiled index slicing drops to `~0.70`, serving as our negative control).
4. **Q-GaLore INT4 Stochastic-Rounded Projectors, Lazy Cosine-Similarity Schedule & 2-Step QR Subspace Iteration (`qat_galore_engine/galore_async_offload.py`):**
   * Upgraded `LayerWise8BitGaLoreEngine` with packed INT4 projection storage (`quantize_int4_stochastic`), calibrated cosine-similarity lazy subspace interval doubling (`calibrate_lazy_svd_threshold`, showing `0.90` skips `80%` of SVDs while preserving `>0.98` trajectory cosine similarity `O15`), sign-preserving QR orthogonal moment reprojection (`O14`, `F20`), and warm-started 2-step QR power iteration (`svd_free_subspace_iteration`).

# 11. Adversarial Review, Verified Status and Known Limitations

This document is the honest ledger for the project. It records what an adversarial logic and code review found, what was fixed, what is still open, and exactly what has and has not been executed. It supersedes any stronger wording in docs 08 to 10 where the two disagree.

## 0. 2026-10-30 development pass (host CPU only; no GPU, driver, or firmware action)

A second pass fixed several review items and audited cross-file consistency. Everything below was built and run on the host CPU with gcc/g++/clang/python; the project is now under local git (baseline commit is the pre-pass snapshot, each round is its own commit). No GPU kernel was compiled or run, no driver patch applied, no shim loaded, no firmware/MMIO touched, and no system setting changed.

* **O8 fixed** - `SmartTieredCache` now defaults to exact (tau = 0). The lossy relative-energy sieve is opt-in; a default-constructed cache is bit-identical to the plain FP32 GEMM (test [8]).
* **O9 fixed** - new `set_error_budget(eps)`: a K-group is skipped only while a worst-case per-output bound (max column |scale| x max row L1 of the activations) keeps the total skipped mass <= eps. Holds for W1 and W2; non-finite activations are never skipped. Verified error <= eps on both; the old summed-energy heuristic breaks the same budget on a single-loud-row input (negative control, test [8]).
* **O11 fixed** - panel promotion counts once per GEMM call, not once per K-group.
* **O14 fixed** - on a GaLore projector refresh the Adam moments are rotated into the new basis (M exactly via P_new^T P_old; V via the squared rotation).
* **GaLore numerical hardening** - 8-bit sqrt(V) now rounds up on quantization so a small coordinate next to a large row max can no longer become 0 and spike the next step; BF16 master-weight writes use unbiased stochastic rounding so sub-ulp updates (e.g. lr 2e-4 on ~0.02 weights) are no longer silently dropped.
* **Memory table corrected** - `compute_32b_qat_memory_table` now derives each peak as sum(components)/n_gpus instead of a hard-coded figure. The GaLore peak was 22.15 but its own components sum to 23.63 GB/GPU; the honest 23.63 still fits a real 24 GiB (= 25.77 decimal GB) card. Host-staged HugePage weights and streamed optimizer moments are explicitly excluded from VRAM.
* **Test-output honesty** - the kinesis "56.89x bandwidth" line is relabelled as a modelled ratio (compression x 1/(1 - skipped)), not a measurement.
* **Sizing-contract audit** - group size 128, weights-per-uint32 (32 for W1, 16 for W2), the Nr=8/Mr=4 CPU micro-tile, and FP32 accumulation are consistent across `unified_sizing.h`, the C++ engine, the PTX emulator and the Python kernels. The C++ constants already derive from the header; the previously free-standing tile literals in `w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu` are now pinned to the header with `static_assert`s (and in Wave 2B/Wave 3, the full `.cu` kernel bodies are also compiled and executed on the host via the 256-fiber CTA / 32-lane warp engine, `O1`).
* **Sanitizers** - the C++ suite, the PTX emulator, the governor and the AMX worker run clean under AddressSanitizer + UBSan.

Scope note: in the initial 2026-10-30 pass, the device-ID interposer (`compiler_unlock/`) and the firmware/driver unlock code (`kernel_driver_patch/`) were left at baseline; subsequent passes (Sections `0.1` and `0.2`) hardened both with thread-local + backtrace caller gating (`O2`), post-FLR `SS0` verification (`O4`), and PCIe topology/ACS/IOMMU verification (`O5`).

## 0.1 2026-11-04 Wave 2B Evaluation & Algorithmic Hardening Pass

An independent technical evaluation of the adversarial review (findings `F1..F12` and open items `O1..O17`) verified full agreement with `F1..F12` and `O8/O9/O11/O14`, uncovered three deeper algorithmic bugs/inefficiencies missed or introduced during the earlier review (`F13..F15`), and resolved the remaining host-verifiable open items (`O1` W2 tail guard, `O4`, `O9` GPU warp reduction, `O10` VNNI scatter, `O12`, and `O14` contraction normalization):

* **F13 fixed (Critical Sign Bug in `cayley_sgd_stiefel_step` + Factorized Kronecker-Stiefel Optimization in `spinquant_packer.py`)** - The original `cayley_sgd_stiefel_step` computed `(I - (lr/2)*Y)^(-1) (I + (lr/2)*Y) R` with `Y = G R^T - R G^T`. Because `<G, (G R^T - R G^T) R> = 0.5 * ||Y||_F^2 >= 0`, that update moved in the **Riemannian gradient ascent** direction, causing activation kurtosis to climb from the Hadamard initialization (`2.95`) up to `19.35`. Fixing the Cayley step to true Riemannian gradient descent `(I + (lr/2)*Y)^(-1) (I - (lr/2)*Y) R` reduces kurtosis from `25.00` down to `1.82`. Also implemented `optimize_kronecker_stiefel_rotation` learning `R_kron = R_P (x) H_16 = (R_P (x) I_16)(I_P (x) H_16)` on `O(K/16)`, where `(I_P (x) H_16)` is executed online by the kernel's `mode=1` HadaCore prologue and `(R_P (x) I_16)` is absorbed into the preceding projection (reducing kurtosis from `25.00 -> 3.65 (fixed H_16) -> 2.44 (learned R_P)` with `3.15e-05` match error).
* **F14 fixed (Hoisted 32-Lane 256-Bit `vpshufb` 4-Panel T-MAC LUT Loop + W2 Zero-Group Scale in `sub2bit_gemm_and_smart_cache.hpp`)** - The earlier `execute_tmac_lut_w1_gemm` implementation rebuilt `lo_bytes[16]` and `hi_bytes[16]` inside the inner panel loop `p` and only used 8 of 16 bytes in a 128-bit `_mm_shuffle_epi8`. Hoisting `lut_lo_256[32]` and `lut_hi_256[32]` (`_mm256_broadcastsi128_si256`) outside the panel loop and processing 4 panels (`32` columns) simultaneously via 256-bit `_mm256_shuffle_epi8` achieves 100% 32-lane `vpshufb` utilization with 0 bit mismatches against the scalar LUT reference. Also fixed `pack_w2_gotoblas_panels` so all-zero ternary weight groups store `scale = 0.0f` instead of `1.0f`.
* **F15 fixed (Salience-Profiled LServe Streaming Head Selection in `lserve_sparse_attn.py`)** - Added `profile_streaming_head_mask` ranking attention heads by their out-of-window vs. in-window bounding-box centroid salience (per LServe, `arXiv:2502.14866`) instead of blind head-index slicing. At 50% streaming heads (`avg_active_pages = 4.5`), salience-profiled head assignment preserves `0.99305` cosine similarity vs. dense FP32 reference.
* **F16 fixed (`K < 128` / `K % 128 != 0` Scale & Weight Stride Truncation in `w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu`)** - All three `__global__` kernels (`fused_w1a16_hadacore_gemm_kernel`, `fused_w2a16_hadacore_gemm_kernel`, and `marlin_streamk_w1a16_decode_kernel`) previously indexed `scales_bf16[col * (K >> 7) + ...]`, `w_packed_u32[col * (K >> 5) + ...]`, and `w2_packed_u32[col * (K >> 4) + ...]`. Whenever `K < 128` (e.g., `K = 32, 48, 64`) or `K % 128 != 0`, `K >> 7` truncated to `0` (or under-counted the `g128` groups per column), reading column 0's scale for every column. Fixed all three kernels to use ceiling strides `((K + 127) >> 7)`, `((K + 31) >> 5)`, and `((K + 15) >> 4)`.
* **O1 (Host 256-Fiber CTA / 32-Lane Warp-Collective Compilation & Odd-`(M, N, K)` Verification of `w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu` + W2 Tail Guard) fixed on host** - Added `(k_base + 16) < K` bounds check in `fused_w2a16_hadacore_gemm_kernel` (`w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu`) so `K % 32 == 16` tails pad with ternary zero (`0x55555555U`), and built a deterministic 2-level (Warp-first, CTA-second) 256-fiber execution engine (`fused_w1_hadacore/w1a16_host_cta_shim.hpp` + `fused_w1_hadacore/w1a16_host_verify_main.hpp`) that compiles and executes the unmodified `__global__` CUDA kernels on the host CPU (`g++ -std=c++20 -O2 -Wall -Wextra -DSTANDALONE_VERIFY -x c++`). Verified 0 bit mismatches across 41 aligned and odd/unaligned `(M, N, K)` test cases (`M = 1, 5, 7, 15, 16, 17, 63, 65, 128, 131`, `N = 1, 9, 63, 64, 65, 67, 71, 127, 129, 131, 192, 256, 259`, `K = 32, 48, 64, 128`), all 3 online HadaCore modes (`0, 1, 2`), `marlin_streamk_w1a16_decode_kernel` (`split_k = 1, 2, 4`), and inter-warp divergent `warp_sieve_k32_active` gating. Also added [`run_all_tests.py`](file:///d:/Coding_Projects/.Unified_Hardware_Accel/run_all_tests.py) to compile and execute all 13 verification suites + repository hygiene audit in a single command.
* **O4 (Post-FLR `SS0`/`SS1` latch verification on S3 resume) fixed** - `nv_pci_resume_early_unlock` (`nv_gpu_ops_and_s3_unlock.c`) now inspects `pdev->ss0_reg` after `pcie_flr(pdev)` + `pci_restore_state(pdev)` and automatically re-executes `gpu_unlock_execute_falcon_payload` post-FLR if the hardware FLR reset the speed-selector latches back to `GU_SS0_LOCKED_DEFAULT` (verified in test `[3b]`).
* **O9 (GPU Warp-Level Exact Row-Maximum Holder Bound) fixed** - Implemented `warp_reduce_rowmax_l1_k32` and `tiled_cbc_subtile_rowmax_bound_mask` in `structural_filters/tiled_cbc_sieve.cu` using intra-row `__shfl_xor_sync` sums (`xor` offsets `1, 2`) and inter-row maxima (`xor` offsets `4, 8, 16`) to compute `max_{m in 0..63} ||A[m, k32]||_1 * alpha_max` (verified against a single-outlier-row test `[4]`).
* **O10 (Vectorized 32-Bit VNNI Pair Scatter in `chorus_amx_moe.c`) fixed** - Replaced the temporary `col_vals[32]` buffer and 16-bit scalar transpose with `_mm512_i32scatter_epi32` (AVX-512 path) and direct 32-bit VNNI pair packing `b0 | (b1 << 16)` (portable reference path), plus cross-platform 1 GB capacity bounds checking in `allocate_pinned_1gb_ring`.
* **O12 (Full 2-Stage Kronecker `H_2 x H_16` Tensor Core Emulation in `ptx_bf162_emu.c`) fixed** - Chained Stage 1 (`ptx_mma_m16n8k16_bf16_f32_warp` right-multiplying each `K=16` sub-tile by normalized `0.25 * H_16`) and Stage 2 (in-register `ptx_fma_rn_bf162` left-multiplying by `H_2`) into Stage 3 (W2A16 ternary MMA), verifying 0 bit mismatches across all 128 warp accumulator elements.
* **O14 (Orthonormal Contraction Moment Transport in `galore_async_offload.py`) refined** - Computed the basis-change matrix `T = P_new_orth^T @ P_old_orth` using sign-preserving QR orthonormalization of `P_old` and orthonormal `P_new_orth` prior to INT4/INT8 projector quantization so `(T * T) @ V_old` is guaranteed non-expansive (`||T||_2 <= 1`) without column sign flips (`F20`).

## 0.2 2026-11-05 Wave 3 Build & Full-Stack Adversarial Audit Pass

Wave 3 completed the remaining multi-pillar integration, real OS multi-threaded SPSC ring worker, and Q-GaLore/QAT numerical extensions, followed by an aggressive 3-domain full-stack adversarial audit (`F17..F20`) across all 18 verification suites (`python run_all_tests.py`):

* **Wave 3A (`e2e_transformer_pipeline.py`, Suite `G1`)** - Built an end-to-end 5-Pillar Transformer Block & QAT verification pipeline chaining: (1) LServe INT4 Hadamard sparse attention with salience-profiled streaming heads (`0.99491` cosine similarity vs. dense FP32), (2) true offline Kronecker-Stiefel projection absorption $W_{O,\text{absorbed}} = (R_P^\top \otimes I_{16}) W_O$ reducing post-$W_O$ activation kurtosis from `24.42 -> 2.76 (H_16) -> 2.09 (learned R_P)` with zero extra runtime GEMM (`3.01e-03` BF16 relative match error), (3) `SmartTieredCache` W1 (`1.125 bpw`) and W2 (`2.125 bpw`) GEMM verified both bit-exact against reference (`0.00e+00`) and against unrotated FP32 ground-truth projections (`W1 cos = 0.8709`, `W2 cos = 0.9457`), (4) closed-loop Chorus + NVRM governor step (`tau = 0.1050`), and (5) multi-step `FusedQATG128Linear` + `LayerWise8BitGaLoreEngine` distillation (`loss 0.2569 -> 0.1612`, `0 Bytes` peak `p.grad`).
* **Wave 3B (`O10` in `hetero_runtime/chorus_amx_moe.c`, Suite `E1`)** - Implemented a real OS worker thread (`_beginthreadex` on Windows, `pthread_create` on POSIX) draining the 64B-aligned `HeteroMoERingSlot` ring concurrently with a pipelined producer (`Completed=8/8 | Payload Err=0 | Final Delta_Q=0`), added OS physical page-locking (`SetProcessWorkingSetSize` + `VirtualLock` / `mlock`) with 64-byte aligned fallback (`_aligned_malloc` / `posix_memalign`), and enforced Intel AMX `TDPBF16PS` BF16 input and FP32 output denormal flush-to-zero (`bf16_to_fp32_amx_ftz`, `fp32_amx_ftz`).
* **Wave 3C (`O15` in `galore_async_offload.py`, Clipped STE in `fused_qat_ste.py`, & `O9` in `tiled_cbc_sieve.py`)** - Added `calibrate_lazy_svd_threshold` benchmarking thresholds `{0.00, 0.85, 0.90, 0.95, 1.00}` against exact step-by-step SVD (`0.90` skips `80%` of SVDs while preserving `0.9811` weight-delta cosine similarity), added optional Clipped STE gating $\mathbb{I}(|w| \le c \cdot \alpha_g)$ in `FusedQATG128Function`, and upgraded `tiled_cbc_sieve.py` to the exact row-maximum Holder bound (`O9`) with a 1-loud-row / 63-zero-row negative control.
* **F17 fixed (Host Shim `0xAE` Truth Table + `pack_w1_interleaved_u32` Sign Bit Polarity + `K % 32 == 16` Tail Guard in `w1a16_host_cta_shim.hpp`, `w1a16_host_verify_main.hpp`, and `w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu`)** - Previously, `host_ptx_lop3_b32` used `(a & b) | c` for both `0xAE` and `0xEA` while `pack_w1_interleaved_u32` in `w1a16_host_verify_main.hpp` inverted its sign bit (`signs < 0 -> 1`), a double-inversion that canceled internally in Suite `B3` while disagreeing with `ptx_bf162_emu.c` and `spinquant_packer.py` (`signs > 0 -> 1`). Fixed `host_ptx_lop3_b32` so `0xAE` evaluates `(~a & b) | c` and `0xEA` evaluates `(a & b) | c`, unified `pack_w1_interleaved_u32` to `signs > 0 -> 1`, and added `has_sub1 = ((k_base + 16) < K)` tail-tile guards for `K % 32 == 16` across both `fused_w1a16_hadacore_gemm_kernel` and `fused_w2a16_hadacore_gemm_kernel` (verified across 45 test cases including `(17, 67, 48, mode=2)`).
* **F18 fixed (Orthonormal $\pm 1/\sqrt{2}$ Kronecker $H_2$ Scaling in `ptx_bf162_emu.c` and `smsp_roofline_sim.py`)** - Stage 2 of the Kronecker $H_{32} = H_2 \otimes H_{16}$ test in `ptx_bf162_emu.c` previously used `0x3F003F00U` (`+0.5f`), giving $H_{32}$ a norm scale of $0.5 \times 0.25 = 1/8 = 1/\sqrt{64}$ instead of orthonormal $1/\sqrt{32}$. Updated `ptx_bf162_emu.c` and `smsp_roofline_sim.py` to use `0x3F353F35U` / `0xBF35BF35U` ($\pm 1/\sqrt{2} \approx \pm 0.70703125_{\text{bf16}}$), matching `w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu:284-285`.
* **F19 fixed (True 32-Lane Warp-Collective Execution of `tiled_cbc_sieve.cu` in Suite `C1`)** - Removed the `#ifdef __CUDACC__` guard that hid the `__device__` functions during host compilation of `structural_filters/tiled_cbc_sieve.cu`, added `__ballot_sync` (`HOST_COLL_BALLOT`) to `w1a16_host_cta_shim.hpp`, updated `tiled_cbc_sieve_pred` to use `warp_reduce_rowmax_l1_k32`, and executed the actual `__device__` functions across 32 warp fiber lanes in `test_tiled_cbc.exe`.
* **F20 fixed (Sign-Preserving QR Orthonormalization of `p_old` & INT4 Column Normalization in `galore_async_offload.py` + `kinesis_unified` Safety Guards)** - Plain Householder QR `torch.linalg.qr(p_old)` can flip column signs ($R_{ii} < 0$), causing `_reproject_moments` to negate `M_old`. Enforced $\text{diag}(R) > 0$ (`q_qr * sign(diag(r_qr))`) on `p_old_orth` and column-normalized `_load_projector` after INT4 dequantization. Also added signed-stride overflow checks (`Shape::contiguous_strides`), `SlabAllocator` double-free protection (`is_free_` bitmap), and `VirtualMemory`/`SlabAllocator` multiplication overflow guards in `kinesis_unified/`.

## 1. What can and cannot be verified in this environment

| Component | How it was exercised | What that proves | What it does NOT prove |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `kinesis_unified` (C++20, AVX2) | Compiled and run on the host CPU | Numerical correctness of packing, FP32 GEMM, W1/W2 unpack, T-MAC LUT vs FP32 | Performance on any target, AVX-512 or AMX paths |
| `sim_harness/ptx_bf162_emu.c` | Host C emulator of PTX semantics | Unpack constants and bit layouts are self-consistent under the emulated semantics | That real hardware or `ptxas` accepts the PTX (see finding F1) |
| `fused_w1_hadacore/*.cu`, `structural_filters/tiled_cbc_sieve.cu` device code | **Compiled and run on host CPU** via 256-fiber CTA / 32-lane Warp-Collective execution shim (`w1a16_host_cta_shim.hpp`, `-Wall -Wextra`, 0 warnings) | Bit-exact correctness of `__global__` CTA/warp tiling, `Swizzle<3,3,3>` shared memory, `ldmatrix.x4`, `mma.m16n8k16`, online HadaCore (`H_16` & `H_32`), Stream-K decode, `__shfl_xor_sync` / `__ballot_sync` row-max sieve, and odd `(M, N, K)` tail guards across 45 test cases | Hardware `nvcc`/`ptxas` register count, occupancy, and real GPU execution speed |
| `hetero_runtime/chorus_amx_moe.c` | Compiled and run on host CPU with real OS worker thread (`_beginthreadex`/`pthread_create`), `VirtualLock`/`mlock`, and AMX FTZ emulation | Layout indexing, 64B ring-slot cache-line isolation, multi-threaded SPSC lock-free ring handshake (`8/8` slots), and AMX BF16/FP32 denormal flush-to-zero semantics | Real hardware `_tile_dpbf16ps` silicon execution and Linux 1GB `MAP_HUGETLB` allocation on a Xeon host |
| `kernel_driver_patch/*.c` | Host mock of NVIDIA driver types | Logic of our own mock (8-slot variable-size LRU lifetime, overlap checks, ACS/IOMMU topology gate, PLM sequence, post-FLR `SS0` re-verify) | Anything about the real NVIDIA driver, GSP firmware, or hardware latches |
| `kernel_driver_patch/*.patch` | Synchronized reference patches audited for syntax/logic | Consistency of patch hunks with `nv_gpu_ops_and_s3_unlock.c` (`O3`, `O4`, `O5`) | That they apply cleanly to every upstream `open-gpu-kernel-modules` tag without porting |
| `compiler_unlock/libcublas_unlock_shim.c` | Host build with a mock driver table + `NVOS54_PARAMETERS` packet verifier | Resolver self-interception (`F12`), ID mapping logic, and thread-local + backtrace caller-aware `NV2080_CTRL_CMD_BUS_GET_PCI_INFO` spoofing (`O2`) | Live `LD_PRELOAD` against closed-source Linux `libcuda.so.1` and `/dev/nvidiactl` |
| `sim_harness/smsp_roofline_sim.py` | Python | Arithmetic of a model with stated assumptions | Any measured speedup |
| Python QAT/GaLore/LServe | CPU PyTorch | Algorithms on small synthetic tensors | Behaviour on real models or GPUs |

Nothing in this repository has been run against a real GPU. Every performance figure is either a model output or taken from a cited external source.

## 2. Findings from the adversarial review

Severity: **S1** wrong results or will not build, **S2** unsafe or silently misleading, **S3** performance or hygiene.

### 2.1 Fixed

| ID | Sev | Finding | Fix |
| :-- | :-- | :--- | :--- |
| F1 | S1 | The kernel used `mul.rn.bf162`, `fma.rn.bf162`, `add.rn.bf162`. These are not PTX mnemonics (the type is `bf16x2`), and `mul`/`add` on `bf16x2` need sm_90, so the kernel could not assemble for sm_86/sm_89. The emulator hid this because it only models semantics. | All device PTX now uses `fma.rn.bf16x2`. A multiply is `fma(a, b, -0.0)` and an add is `fma(x, 1.0, y)`; both are bit-identical to the IEEE operation under round-to-nearest and valid on sm_80+. Constants are moved into registers instead of relying on immediates. |
| F2 | S1 | Kronecker butterfly inline asm declared outputs `"=r"` while later instructions still read inputs. The allocator may alias an output with an input, corrupting `t1` mid-sequence. | Every output is early-clobber `"=&r"`. |
| F3 | S1 | `ldmatrix.sync.aligned` was guarded by a per-lane condition (`r_bot < M` with `r_bot = group_id + 8 + ...`). It is a warp collective; divergent execution is undefined and can hang. | Guard is now warp-uniform: `(block_row + mi * 16 + 15) < M`. |
| F4 | S1 | The kernel rotates activations with `I (x) H16` (or `I (x) H2 (x) H16`) but `spinquant_packer.py` merged a dense Cayley or full-size Hadamard rotation into the weights. `(xR1)(WR2)^T != xW^T` unless `R1 == R2`, so end-to-end outputs would be wrong. | Added `build_kernel_online_rotation(K, mode)` that returns exactly what the kernel applies. Tests prove `(xR)(WR)^T = xW^T` to 2e-5 and include a negative control showing the mixed case is off by about 58. Rule: a learned dense rotation is only valid when absorbed offline into neighbouring layers, never as the online rotation. |
| F5 | S1 | `pack_w1_gotoblas_panels` forced the scale of an all-zero group to 1.0, so zero weights produced `+/- sum(activations)` instead of 0. | Zero groups keep scale 0; regression test added. |
| F6 | S2 | `ioctl` hook matched only `_IOC_NR == 0x2A` and then treated the argument as an `NVOS54_PARAMETERS`. Any unrelated ioctl with NR 0x2A (DRM, V4L2, ...) would be read and possibly written as that struct. | Also requires `_IOC_TYPE == 'F'` (NVRM magic). (Linux only; not compiled in this environment.) |
| F7 | S2 | The dynamic BAR1 LRU could evict or overwrite a slot that a live P2P mapping still used, giving two buffers the same BAR1 window (silent data corruption), orphaned slots in the bump path, and no unmap at all. | Reference counting; reclaim only unreferenced slots of identical size; `kbusUnmapFbAperture_HAL` and `nvGpuOpsDestroyExternalAllocPtes_Unified`; tests for full aperture refusal, release and reuse, double unmap. |
| F8 | S2 | `allocate_pinned_1gb_ring` could overflow `num_slots * sizeof` and memset past the single 1 GB mapping. | Overflow and size checks; falls back to the heap for oversize requests. |
| F9 | S1 | AMX code never requested XTILEDATA permission. On Linux 5.16+ the first tile instruction raises SIGILL without `arch_prctl(ARCH_REQ_XCOMP_PERM, 18)`. | `amx_request_permission()` called from `amx_init_tiles_bf16()`, which now returns a status. Per-thread configuration is documented. (Linux AMX only; not executable here.) |
| F10 | S3 | Sieve accumulated 32 values in bf16, so rounding error compounded. The L1 norm is also not invariant under the Hadamard rotation (only L2 is), which a doc claimed. | bf16 only for 4-term partial sums, FP32 accumulation after; invariance claim corrected in code comments. |
| F11 | S3 | Governor divided by `locked_clock_floor_mhz` with no guard. | Guard added. |
| F12 | S2 | Shim answered `cuGetProcAddress(_v2)` lookups only for two symbols. CUDA 12.x asks the resolver for itself and then uses that raw pointer, bypassing the shim. | Resolver names are intercepted via a shared helper; test confirms the pointer handed back is the hook and still spoofs correctly. |
| F13 | S1 | `cayley_sgd_stiefel_step` in `spinquant_packer.py` used `(I - (lr/2)*Y)^(-1) (I + (lr/2)*Y) R` with `Y = G R^T - R G^T`, which moves in the Riemannian gradient **ascent** direction (`<G, Y R> = 0.5*||Y||_F^2 >= 0`). | Fixed to `(I + (lr/2)*Y)^(-1) (I - (lr/2)*Y) R` (true Riemannian gradient descent, dropping kurtosis from `25.00` to `1.82`) and added `optimize_kronecker_stiefel_rotation` learning `R_kron = R_P (x) H_16` (`25.00 -> 3.65 -> 2.44`). |
| F14 | S2 | `execute_tmac_lut_w1_gemm` rebuilt `lo_bytes[16]`/`hi_bytes[16]` inside the panel loop `p` and only used 8 of 16 bytes in a 128-bit `pshufb`; `pack_w2_gotoblas_panels` gave all-zero ternary groups `scale = 1.0f`. | Hoisted `lut_lo_256[32]`/`lut_hi_256[32]` outside the panel loop and added a 4-panel (`32`-column) full 32-lane 256-bit `_mm256_shuffle_epi8` loop (0 bit mismatches vs. scalar LUT); zero ternary groups now store `scale = 0.0f`. |
| F15 | S2 | `lserve_sparse_hadamard_attention` selected streaming heads blindly by head index `0..n_stream-1`, dropping cosine similarity to `0.70` when those heads carried middle-of-context needles. | Added `profile_streaming_head_mask` selecting streaming heads by out-of-window vs. in-window centroid salience gap (`0.99305` cosine similarity at 50% streaming heads). |
| F16 | S1 | `w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu` indexed `scales_bf16` with `col * (K >> 7)` and packed weights with `col * (K >> 5)` / `col * (K >> 4)`, which truncated to `0` (or under-counted strides) whenever `K < 128` or `K % 128 != 0`. | Replaced with ceiling strides `((K + 127) >> 7)`, `((K + 31) >> 5)`, and `((K + 15) >> 4)` across all three `__global__` kernels; verified on `K = 32, 48, 64, 128`. |
| F17 | S1 | `host_ptx_lop3_b32` in `w1a16_host_cta_shim.hpp` evaluated `(a & b) | c` for `0xAE` instead of `(~a & b) | c`, masked by an inverted sign-bit convention in `w1a16_host_verify_main.hpp`; `K % 32 == 16` tails in `mode=2` mixed out-of-bounds `sub1` in `H_2`. | Fixed `host_ptx_lop3_b32` `0xAE` to `(~a & b) | c`, unified `pack_w1_interleaved_u32` sign polarity (`> 0 -> 1`) across all files, and added `has_sub1 = ((k_base + 16) < K)` tail guards in both W1 and W2 kernels (verified across 45 cases). |
| F18 | S1 | `ptx_bf162_emu.c` Stage 2 Kronecker $H_2$ butterfly used `0x3F003F00U` (`0.5f`) instead of orthonormal `1/sqrt(2)` (`0x3F353F35U`). | Updated `ptx_bf162_emu.c` and `smsp_roofline_sim.py` to `0x3F353F35U` / `0xBF35BF35U` ($\pm 0.70703125_{\text{bf16}}$), matching `w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu`. |
| F19 | S2 | `structural_filters/tiled_cbc_sieve.cu` hid `__device__` functions behind `#ifdef __CUDACC__` during host compilation and tested a scalar duplicate in `main()`. | Included `w1a16_host_cta_shim.hpp` (with `__ballot_sync` emulation) in `tiled_cbc_sieve.cu` and executed the real `__device__` functions across 32 warp fiber lanes. |
| F20 | S1 | Householder QR `torch.linalg.qr(p_old)` in `_reproject_moments` (`galore_async_offload.py`) could flip column signs ($R_{ii} < 0$), negating `M_old` on projector refresh. | Enforced positive diagonal entries on $R$ (`p_old_orth = q_qr * sign(diag(r_qr))`) and column-normalized INT4 projectors on load (`0.9811` trajectory cosine similarity). |

### 2.2 Status of Open & Hardware-Dependent Items (`O1..O17`)

| ID | Sev | Finding | Notes |
| :-- | :-- | :--- | :--- |
| **O1 (Host CTA/Warp Verified; HW `nvcc` pending)** | S1 | Device code had not been compiled or tested on odd `(M, N, K)` shapes, and W2 loads read `k_base/16 + 1` words out of range when `K % 32 != 0`. | Both `.cu` files now compile cleanly on the host (`g++ -std=c++20 -Wall -Wextra -x c++`) and run all `__global__` and `__device__` kernels through a 256-fiber CTA / 32-lane Warp-Collective execution engine across 45 aligned and odd-`(M, N, K)` cases with 0 bit mismatches. `fused_w2a16_hadacore_gemm_kernel` also uses double-buffered `Swizzle<3,3,3>` + `ldmatrix_x4_bf16`. Hardware `nvcc -arch=sm_86 -arch=sm_89` register/occupancy check remains for a GPU host. |
| **O2 (FIXED 2026-11-05)** | S1 | The ioctl-level spoof (`shim_spoof_nvos54_packet`) was process-global because `ioctl` runs inside `libcuda`. | Added thread-local cuBLAS call-depth tracking (`shim_enter_cublas_context` / `shim_leave_cublas_context`) and multi-frame backtrace `dladdr` inspection (`is_callstack_cublas`) in `libcublas_unlock_shim.c` so `shim_spoof_nvos54_packet` only spoofs `NVOS54_PARAMETERS` when `libcublas` / `libcublasLt` / `libcudnn` is on the active call chain (verified with negative/positive controls in `test_cublas_shim.c`). |
| **O3 (Patches Synchronized; HW Driver Tree Pending)** | S1 | `.patch` files are unapplied sketches, and patch 02 called the map function with no matching unmap or LRU. | Synchronized `01-gsp-and-static-bar1-p2p.patch`, `02-dynamic-bar1-48gb-and-noncoh-pte.patch`, and `03-falcon-ss0-unlock-and-s3-resume.patch` with overflow-safe bounds checks, `pRemoteGpu` ownership, `nvGpuOpsDestroyExternalAllocPtes` / `kbusUnmapFbAperture_HAL` teardown, and post-FLR `SS0` verification. Porting against a live `open-gpu-kernel-modules` checkout remains for target hardware. |
| **O4 (FIXED in S3 Hook; HW test pending)** | S1 | The S3 resume hook assumes SS0/SS1 override latches survive a PCIe FLR. No evidence in this repo; FLR may reset them, which would undo the unlock. | `nv_pci_resume_early_unlock` now checks `pdev->ss0_reg` post-FLR and automatically re-executes `gpu_unlock_execute_falcon_payload` if FLR reset the latches (test `[3b]`). Real hardware validation still required. |
| **O5 (FIXED 2026-11-05)** | S2 | Patch 01 returned "P2P supported" for any GPU whose BAR1 is at least 1 GB without checking root complex, ACS, or IOMMU mode. | Added `kbusIsPcieBar1P2PMappingSupported_Unified` in `nv_gpu_ops_and_s3_unlock.c` and Patch `01` validating PCIe domain/root-complex affinity, upstream bridge ACS `PCI_ACS_RR | PCI_ACS_CR | PCI_ACS_TB` bits, and strict IOMMU blocking before admitting BAR1 P2P (verified by negative controls in test `[1b]`). |
| O6 | S2 | The "isolated IOMMU peer domain" function is an arithmetic checker. It does not program any IOMMU, ACS or access policy, so it does not make devices "only talk to each other". Likewise the VBIOS "rollback" is a struct update; nothing touches flash. | Real enforcement is IOMMU group policy plus ACS; real rollback is a verified flash of the stock image. Neither is implemented in the host mock. |
| O7 | S2 | `ACS` redirect clearing exists only as an opt-in helper in the mock, never auto-invoked, because it weakens isolation. | Prefer `pci=config_acs=` kernel parameters. |
| **O8 (FIXED 2026-10-30)** | S2 | Tiled-CBC sieve and the default `energy_sieve_tau = 0.85` in `SmartTieredCache` are LOSSY. A caller using defaults silently gets approximate results. The "effective bandwidth amplification" in test output (for example 56.89x) is a computed ratio (compression x 1/(1 - pruned)), not a measurement. | Exactness (`tau = 0`) is now the default across `SmartTieredCache` and `e2e_transformer_pipeline.py`; pruning requires explicit opt-in (`set_error_budget(eps)` or `set_energy_sieve_threshold(tau)`). |
| **O9 (FIXED Everywhere 2026-11-05)** | S2 | The scale-weighted sub-tile gate used mean row L1, an average-case estimate, not a worst-case bound. | Fixed across all four locations: C++ (`SmartTieredCache::set_error_budget`), standalone CUDA sieve (`warp_reduce_rowmax_l1_k32` in `tiled_cbc_sieve.cu`), Python sieve (`tiled_cbc_sieve.py`), and the fused in-register GEMM gate (`warp_sieve_k32_active` in `w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu`, verified with a 1-loud-row / 63-zero-row negative control). |
| **O10 (FIXED on Host; HW AMX silicon pending)** | S3 | `chorus_amx_moe.c` unpacked weights with a scalar 16x32 store loop per K step, had no consumer thread or page-locking, and used plain float arithmetic without AMX denormal flushing. | Vectorised with `_mm512_i32scatter_epi32` (AVX-512 path) and strict-aliasing-clean 32-bit VNNI pair packing, added `VirtualLock`/`mlock` page-locking, added a real OS consumer thread (`_beginthreadex`/`pthread_create`) draining the 64B-isolated SPSC ring (`8/8` slots), and enforced Intel AMX `TDPBF16PS` BF16/FP32 denormal flush-to-zero (`bf16_to_fp32_amx_ftz`, `fp32_amx_ftz`). Silicon AMX execution remains for a Xeon host. |
| **O11 (FIXED 2026-10-30)** | S3 | `record_panel_access_and_promote` is called once per (panel, group), so promotion counts scale with group count rather than GEMM calls; victim search is O(panels) per call. | Count per GEMM, saturating at `UINT32_MAX`. |
| **O12 (FIXED 2026-11-05)** | S3 | The `ptx_bf162_emu.c` Kronecker test checked the H2 mixing and ternary MMA arithmetic without exercising the H16 stage. | Chained Stage 1 (`ptx_mma_m16n8k16_bf16_f32_warp` right-multiplying by `0.25 * H_16`) into Stage 2 (orthonormal $\pm 1/\sqrt{2}$ `H_2` butterfly `0x3F353F35U`, `F18`) and Stage 3 (W2A16 ternary MMA) with 0 bit mismatches across all 128 elements. |
| **O13 (FIXED / Measured 2026-11-05)** | S3 | The two bf16 rounding steps (after H16 and after the H2 butterfly) compound; no end-to-end error budget for rotated activations had been measured. | Added `verify_compounding_bf16_error_budget_o13` (test `[5]` in `w1a16_host_verify_main.hpp`) comparing 2-stage BF16 Kronecker `H_32` against an exact FP64 Walsh-Hadamard oracle on both Gaussian and heavy-tailed (`50x` spike) activations: empirical relative L2 error is `< 0.6%` (well below the theoretical `2 * 2^-7 = 1.56%` bound). |
| **O14 (FIXED 2026-11-05)** | S3 | After a Q-GaLore projector refresh the Adam moments stayed in the old basis. | Moments are re-projected into the new basis via sign-preserving QR orthonormal change-of-basis $T = P_{\text{new,orth}}^\top P_{\text{old,orth}}$ (`F20`). |
| **O15 (FIXED / Calibrated 2026-11-05)** | S3 | The lazy-schedule test forced doubling with threshold 0.0 to test the mechanism; the 0.90 default had not been calibrated in-repo. | Added `calibrate_lazy_svd_threshold` in `galore_async_offload.py` comparing `{0.00, 0.85, 0.90, 0.95, 1.00}` against exact step-by-step SVD (`1.00`): threshold `0.90` skips `80%` of SVDs (`2/10` calls) while preserving `0.9811` weight-delta cosine similarity and matching final loss to `1.1e-4`. |
| **O16 (Deterministic Split-K FIXED; HW benchmark pending)** | S3 | The Stream-K and hetero tables in `smsp_roofline_sim.py` are driven by an assumption (50% of SMs saturate DRAM, DDR5 80 GB/s, PCIe 25 GB/s). Split-K with FP32 `atomicAdd` was also non-deterministic in summation order. | Added optional `splitk_workspace_fp32` + `marlin_streamk_reduce_slices_kernel` to `w1a16_hadacore_kernel.cu` accumulating `[split_k_slices, M, N]` partial sums in fixed slice order `0 .. split_k_slices - 1` (verified bit-exact in test `[3]`). Real hardware throughput measurements remain for a GPU host. |
| O17 | S3 | Unverified device IDs: RTX 4090 D `0x2685`, RTX 5090 `0x2B85`, RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell `0x2BB1` come from community reports. | Confirm on hardware. |

## 3. Historical Claims Reconciled Across Documents

* Earlier drafts of Doc 10 stated that features such as the T-MAC LUT kernel, offline hot-panel seeding, streaming heads, Q-GaLore options, the 4090 D / 5090 mappings, resolver self-interception, and the Stream-K model had been added before they were written. All of those components are now implemented and verified across the 18 host test suites (`A1..G1` + `H1`) to the exact extent shown in Section 1.
* Blind head-index selection (`0..n_stream-1`) at 50% streaming heads dropped cosine similarity to `~0.70` on the planted-needle test; adding salience-profiled head selection (`profile_streaming_head_mask`, `F15`) restores `0.99305` cosine similarity while keeping the unprofiled case as a negative control.
* The average-row L1 sieve heuristic was replaced across C++, CUDA, and Python by the worst-case row-maximum Holder bound (`O9`), with exact computation (`tau = 0`, `O8`) as the default.
* Factorized Kronecker-Stiefel optimization (`optimize_kronecker_stiefel_rotation`, `F13`) is now implemented in `spinquant_packer.py` and verified end-to-end with offline $W_O$ absorption in `e2e_transformer_pipeline.py` (Suite `G1`).
* Caller-aware PCI ID spoofing now covers both direct `libcuda` attribute/name queries and `ioctl` `NV2080_CTRL_CMD_BUS_GET_PCI_INFO` calls via thread-local depth tracking and multi-frame backtrace `dladdr` inspection (`O2`).

## 4. Remaining Hardware Validation Steps (Requires Target Bare-Metal GPU Host)

1. Compile the CUDA files with `nvcc -arch=sm_86 -arch=sm_89` on a machine with the CUDA Toolkit installed and inspect `ptxas -v` register counts and SM occupancy (`O1`).
2. Run the 45-case odd-`(M, N, K)` verification harness (`w1a16_host_verify_main.hpp`) and `e2e_transformer_pipeline.py` on physical Ampere (`GA102`) and Ada (`AD102`) GPUs.
3. Port the synchronized reference patches (`01..03`) to a live `open-gpu-kernel-modules` checkout on a multi-GPU test rig with IOMMU enabled (`O3` to `O7`).
4. Measure physical GPU and Xeon AMX throughput and compare against the analytical SMSP roofline (`smsp_roofline_sim.py`) and modelled compression ratios in Docs 02, 04, 08, and 10.

---

# Complete Source Code & Verification Implementation Tree

## File: `kinesis_unified/unified_sizing.h`
```c
// File: kinesis_unified/unified_sizing.h
// Single Source of Truth for Hardware, Cache, Tile, Quantization, and Memory Sizing
// Shared across C11 (sim_harness, hetero_runtime, kernel_driver_patch),
// CUDA/PTX (fused_w1_hadacore, structural_filters), and C++20 (kinesis_unified).

#ifndef UHA_UNIFIED_SIZING_H
#define UHA_UNIFIED_SIZING_H

#include <stddef.h>
#include <stdint.h>

// ==============================================================================
// 1. Hardware Alignment & OS Virtual Memory Granularities (Bytes)
// ==============================================================================
#define UHA_GPU_SECTOR_BYTES             32U
#define UHA_CPU_CACHELINE_BYTES          64U
#define UHA_GPU_SRAM_SWIZZLE_BYTES       128U
#define UHA_TENSOR_VIEW_DESC_BYTES       128U        // Exact 2 x 64B CPU cache lines / 1 x 128B GPU line
#define UHA_SHAPE_DESC_BYTES             56U
#define UHA_STRIDES_DESC_BYTES           56U
#define UHA_MAX_TENSOR_DIMS              6U

#define UHA_OS_PAGE_BYTES                4096ULL     // 4 KB standard virtual page
#define UHA_WIN_ALLOC_GRANULARITY_BYTES  65536ULL    // 64 KB Windows VirtualAlloc reservation granularity
#define UHA_HUGEPAGE_2MB_BYTES           (2ULL << 20)
#define UHA_HUGEPAGE_1GB_BYTES           (1ULL << 30)

// ==============================================================================
// 2. GPU SM SRAM, Register File & BAR1 Aperture Limits (GA102 sm_86 / AD102 sm_89)
// ==============================================================================
#define UHA_SM_REG_FILE_BYTES            262144U     // 256 KB (65,536 x 32-bit registers per SM)
#define UHA_SM_MAX_REGS_PER_THREAD       255U
#define UHA_SM_L1_SRAM_TOTAL_BYTES       131072U     // 128 KB physical L1/Shared SRAM per SM
#define UHA_SM86_SM89_MAX_DYN_SMEM_BYTES 101376U     // 99 KB (99 * 1024 B) max opt-in dynamic Shared Memory per CTA
#define UHA_SM_SRAM_BANKS                32U         // 32 banks x 4B = 128B per crossbar cycle
#define UHA_SM_SRAM_BYTES_PER_CYCLE      128U
#define UHA_SMSP_PER_SM                  4U
#define UHA_WARP_SIZE                    32U

#define UHA_BAR1_STATIC_APERTURE_BYTES   (32ULL << 30) // 32 GB static BAR1 PCIe aperture ceiling
#define UHA_P2P_PAGE_2MB_BYTES           (2U << 20)    // 2 MB GMMU peer page table entry granularity

// ==============================================================================
// 3. Sub-2-Bit Quantization & HadaCore Subspace Block Sizing
// ==============================================================================
#define UHA_QUANT_GROUP_SIZE             128U        // g = 128 elements per BF16 scale factor
#define UHA_QUANT_GROUP_SHIFT            7U          // log2(128) = 7
#define UHA_HADACORE_BLOCK_DIM           16U         // 16 x 16 Sylvester Walsh-Hadamard block

// W1 (Binary g128): 1 bit/weight + 16-bit BF16 scale per 128 weights = 1.125 bits/weight
// Compression: 32 / 1.125 = 28.444x vs FP32, 16 / 1.125 = 14.222x vs FP16
#define UHA_W1_WEIGHTS_PER_U32           32U
#define UHA_W1_WEIGHTS_PER_U32_SHIFT     5U          // log2(32) = 5
#define UHA_W1_BITS_PER_WEIGHT_NUM       9U          // 9 / 8 = 1.125 bits/weight
#define UHA_W1_BITS_PER_WEIGHT_DEN       8U

// W2 (Ternary b1.58 g128): 2 bits/weight + 16-bit BF16 scale per 128 weights = 2.125 bits/weight
// Compression: 32 / 2.125 = 15.059x vs FP32, 16 / 2.125 = 7.529x vs FP16
#define UHA_W2_WEIGHTS_PER_U32           16U
#define UHA_W2_WEIGHTS_PER_U32_SHIFT     4U          // log2(16) = 4
#define UHA_W2_BITS_PER_WEIGHT_NUM       17U         // 17 / 8 = 2.125 bits/weight
#define UHA_W2_BITS_PER_WEIGHT_DEN       8U

// ==============================================================================
// 4. GPU Threadblock (CTA), Warp-Tile & MMA Instruction Geometry
// ==============================================================================
#define UHA_CTA_WARPS                    8U
#define UHA_CTA_THREADS                  256U        // 8 warps x 32 threads
#define UHA_CTA_BLOCK_M                  128U
#define UHA_CTA_BLOCK_N                  256U
#define UHA_CTA_BLOCK_K                  32U         // 4 stages x (128+256)*32*2B = 98,304 B <= 101,376 B (99 KB)
#define UHA_CTA_PIPELINE_STAGES          4U
#define UHA_CTA_SMEM_STAGE_BYTES         24576U      // (128 + 256) * 32 * 2 bytes = 24 KB per stage
#define UHA_CTA_SMEM_4STAGE_BYTES        98304U      // 4 * 24,576 B = 96 KB <= 101,376 B (99 KB)

#define UHA_WARP_TILE_M                  64U         // 4 x m16 slices
#define UHA_WARP_TILE_N                  64U         // 8 x n8 slices
#define UHA_WARP_TILE_K                  32U         // 2 x k16 sub-steps per uint32 weight word
#define UHA_MMA_M                        16U
#define UHA_MMA_N                        8U
#define UHA_MMA_K                        16U

// ==============================================================================
// 5. CPU GotoBLAS / BLIS AVX2 & Intel AMX Tile Geometry
// ==============================================================================
#define UHA_CPU_AVX2_MR                  4U          // 4 rows x 8 cols = 32 FP32 accumulators in 4 YMM regs
#define UHA_CPU_AVX2_NR                  8U          // 8 FP32 lanes per 256-bit YMM register
#define UHA_CPU_AMX_TILE_M               16U         // 16 rows per AMX tmm tile register
#define UHA_CPU_AMX_TILE_N               16U         // 16 FP32 columns (64 bytes) per AMX tmm accumulator
#define UHA_CPU_AMX_TILE_K               32U         // 32 BF16 elements (64 bytes) per AMX VNNI row

// Default fallback CPU cache sizes when running on non-CPUID platforms
#define UHA_DEFAULT_CPU_L1D_BYTES        32768ULL    // 32 KB
#define UHA_DEFAULT_CPU_L2_BYTES         524288ULL   // 512 KB
#define UHA_DEFAULT_CPU_L3_BYTES         16777216ULL // 16 MB

// ==============================================================================
// 6. Paged KV-Cache (SlabAllocator) & LServe Sparse Centroid Sizing
// ==============================================================================
#define UHA_KV_PAGE_TOKENS               64U         // 64 tokens per KV page
#define UHA_KV_DEFAULT_HEAD_DIM          128U        // Standard 128 head dimension (8 x H_16 blocks)
// Packed INT4 KV page: 64 tokens * 128 head_dim * 4 bits / 8 bits/byte = 4,096 bytes (1 OS page!)
#define UHA_KV_PAGE_INT4_BYTES           4096U
// Unpacked FP16/BF16 KV page: 64 tokens * 128 head_dim * 2 bytes = 16,384 bytes (16 KB)
#define UHA_KV_PAGE_BF16_BYTES           16384U
// Per-page min/max BF16 key centroids: 2 * 128 * 2 bytes = 512 bytes
#define UHA_KV_PAGE_CENTROID_BYTES       512U

// ==============================================================================
// 7. Heterogeneous MoE Ring Buffer Sizing (Chorus + NVRM Governor)
// ==============================================================================
#define UHA_MOE_MAX_BATCH_TOKENS         16U
#define UHA_MOE_TOP_K_EXPERTS            8U
#define UHA_MOE_DEFAULT_HIDDEN_DIM       4096U
#define UHA_MOE_RING_SLOT_PAYLOAD_BYTES  (UHA_MOE_MAX_BATCH_TOKENS * UHA_MOE_DEFAULT_HIDDEN_DIM * 2U) // 128 KB

#endif // UHA_UNIFIED_SIZING_H
```

## File: `kinesis_unified/memory_primitives.hpp`
```cpp
// File: kinesis_unified/memory_primitives.hpp
// Unified & Hardened Memory Subsystem pulled from AI_Library (Kinesis) into .Unified_Hardware_Accel.
// Fixes applied over original AI_Library:
//   1. Prefaulted + Page-Locked OS VirtualMemory (VirtualLock / mlock + 4KB page-stride touch)
//      to eliminate 4x first-touch page-fault latency spikes in hot loops (Design Review §1.10, T1).
//   2. Explicit MemorySpace tagging (HostHeap, HostPinnedLocked, CpuL3Locked, SingleGpuVram)
//      and ahead-of-time WorkspacePlanner so hot loops never allocate or throw (Design Review T1, T13).
//   3. Three-Lifetime Arena Manager (Persistent weights, Step activations/KV, Scratch workspace)
//      solving the two-arena training/inference lifetime conflict (Design Review T2).

#pragma once

#include "unified_sizing.h"
#include <algorithm>
#include <array>
#include <cassert>
#include <concepts>
#include <cstddef>
#include <cstdint>
#include <cstring>
#include <initializer_list>
#include <limits>
#include <new>
#include <span>
#include <stdexcept>
#include <string>
#include <sstream>
#include <type_traits>
#include <utility>
#include <vector>

#if defined(_WIN32) || defined(_WIN64)
#ifndef WIN32_LEAN_AND_MEAN
#define WIN32_LEAN_AND_MEAN
#endif
#ifndef NOMINMAX
#define NOMINMAX
#endif
#include <windows.h>
#else
#include <sys/mman.h>
#include <unistd.h>
#endif

namespace kinesis::unified {

// Hardware alignment constants anchored to unified_sizing.h
inline constexpr size_t kGpuSectorAlignment    = UHA_GPU_SECTOR_BYTES;
inline constexpr size_t kCpuCacheLineAlignment = UHA_CPU_CACHELINE_BYTES;
inline constexpr size_t kGpuSramSwizzleAlign   = UHA_GPU_SRAM_SWIZZLE_BYTES;
inline constexpr size_t kDefaultAlignment      = UHA_CPU_CACHELINE_BYTES;
inline constexpr size_t kMaxDimensions         = UHA_MAX_TENSOR_DIMS;
inline constexpr size_t kKvPageTokens          = UHA_KV_PAGE_TOKENS;
inline constexpr size_t kKvDefaultHeadDim      = UHA_KV_DEFAULT_HEAD_DIM;
inline constexpr size_t kKvPageInt4Bytes       = UHA_KV_PAGE_INT4_BYTES;
inline constexpr size_t kKvPageBf16Bytes       = UHA_KV_PAGE_BF16_BYTES;
inline constexpr size_t kKvPageCentroidBytes   = UHA_KV_PAGE_CENTROID_BYTES;

// Explicit Memory Spaces (Fixes T13: First-class CPU/GPU memory space tracking)
enum class MemorySpace : uint8_t {
    HostHeap          = 0, // Standard 64B-aligned CPU heap
    HostPinnedLocked  = 1, // Prefaulted + OS page-locked (VirtualLock/mlock) zero-copy host RAM
    CpuL3CacheLocked  = 2, // Sub-2-bit micro-panels sized to stay resident in CPU L3 cache
    SingleGpuVram     = 3  // Hot-tier single-GPU device VRAM (or mapped BAR1 slot)
};

[[nodiscard]] constexpr size_t align_up(size_t address, size_t alignment) noexcept {
    return (address + alignment - 1) & ~(alignment - 1);
}

[[nodiscard]] inline bool is_aligned(const void* ptr, size_t alignment) noexcept {
    return (reinterpret_cast<uintptr_t>(ptr) & (alignment - 1)) == 0;
}

// ============================================================================
// 1. Upgraded VirtualMemory with Prefaulting & Physical Page Locking
// ============================================================================

class VirtualMemory {
public:
    [[nodiscard]] static size_t page_size() noexcept {
#if defined(_WIN32) || defined(_WIN64)
        SYSTEM_INFO si;
        GetSystemInfo(&si);
        return static_cast<size_t>(si.dwPageSize);
#else
        return static_cast<size_t>(sysconf(_SC_PAGESIZE));
#endif
    }

    [[nodiscard]] static size_t allocation_granularity() noexcept {
#if defined(_WIN32) || defined(_WIN64)
        SYSTEM_INFO si;
        GetSystemInfo(&si);
        return static_cast<size_t>(si.dwAllocationGranularity);
#else
        return page_size();
#endif
    }

    [[nodiscard]] static void* allocate(size_t bytes) noexcept {
        const size_t pg = page_size();
        if (bytes == 0 || bytes > (std::numeric_limits<size_t>::max() - pg)) return nullptr;
        const size_t aligned_size = align_up(bytes, pg);
#if defined(_WIN32) || defined(_WIN64)
        return VirtualAlloc(nullptr, aligned_size, MEM_RESERVE | MEM_COMMIT, PAGE_READWRITE);
#else
        void* ptr = mmap(nullptr, aligned_size, PROT_READ | PROT_WRITE,
                         MAP_PRIVATE | MAP_ANONYMOUS, -1, 0);
        return (ptr == MAP_FAILED) ? nullptr : ptr;
#endif
    }

    /**
     * @brief Allocates, prefaults every 4KB page into the OS page table, and locks physical
     *        frames in RAM (VirtualLock / mlock) so hot inference loops take zero page faults.
     */
    [[nodiscard]] static void* allocate_locked_prefaulted(size_t bytes, bool* out_locked = nullptr) noexcept {
        const size_t pg = page_size();
        if (bytes == 0 || bytes > (std::numeric_limits<size_t>::max() - pg)) {
            if (out_locked) *out_locked = false;
            return nullptr;
        }
        const size_t aligned_size = align_up(bytes, pg);
        void* ptr = allocate(aligned_size);
        if (!ptr) {
            if (out_locked) *out_locked = false;
            return nullptr;
        }

        // Deterministic page-stride write to prefault all PTEs before entering the hot loop
        volatile auto* byte_ptr = static_cast<volatile std::byte*>(ptr);
        for (size_t offset = 0; offset < aligned_size; offset += pg) {
            byte_ptr[offset] = std::byte{0};
        }

        bool locked = false;
#if defined(_WIN32) || defined(_WIN64)
        locked = (VirtualLock(ptr, aligned_size) != 0);
        if (!locked) {
            SIZE_T min_ws = 0, max_ws = 0;
            if (GetProcessWorkingSetSize(GetCurrentProcess(), &min_ws, &max_ws)) {
                const SIZE_T needed = min_ws + aligned_size + (16ULL << 20);
                if (SetProcessWorkingSetSize(GetCurrentProcess(), needed, std::max(max_ws, needed * 2))) {
                    locked = (VirtualLock(ptr, aligned_size) != 0);
                }
            }
        }
#else
        locked = (mlock(ptr, aligned_size) == 0);
#endif
        if (out_locked) *out_locked = locked;
        return ptr;
    }

    static void free(void* ptr, size_t bytes, bool was_locked = false) noexcept {
        if (!ptr) return;
        const size_t aligned_size = align_up(bytes, page_size());
#if defined(_WIN32) || defined(_WIN64)
        if (was_locked) {
            VirtualUnlock(ptr, aligned_size);
        }
        VirtualFree(ptr, 0, MEM_RELEASE);
#else
        if (was_locked) {
            munlock(ptr, aligned_size);
        }
        munmap(ptr, aligned_size);
#endif
    }
};

// ============================================================================
// 2. Abstract Allocator & Prefaulted ArenaAllocator
// ============================================================================

class Allocator {
public:
    virtual ~Allocator() = default;
    [[nodiscard]] virtual void* allocate(size_t bytes, size_t alignment = kDefaultAlignment) = 0;
    virtual void deallocate(void* ptr, size_t bytes, size_t alignment = kDefaultAlignment) noexcept = 0;
    [[nodiscard]] virtual bool owns(const void* ptr) const noexcept = 0;
    [[nodiscard]] virtual MemorySpace memory_space() const noexcept { return MemorySpace::HostHeap; }
    virtual void reset() noexcept {}
};

class ArenaAllocator final : public Allocator {
public:
    explicit ArenaAllocator(size_t capacityBytes, bool lockAndPrefault = true,
                            MemorySpace space = MemorySpace::HostPinnedLocked)
        : capacity_(capacityBytes), owns_buffer_(true), space_(space) {
        if (capacityBytes > 0) {
            if (lockAndPrefault) {
                buffer_ = static_cast<std::byte*>(
                    VirtualMemory::allocate_locked_prefaulted(capacityBytes, &is_locked_)
                );
            } else {
                buffer_ = static_cast<std::byte*>(VirtualMemory::allocate(capacityBytes));
            }
            if (!buffer_) {
                throw std::bad_alloc();
            }
        }
    }

    explicit ArenaAllocator(std::span<std::byte> externalBuffer,
                            MemorySpace space = MemorySpace::HostHeap) noexcept
        : buffer_(externalBuffer.data()),
          capacity_(externalBuffer.size()),
          current_offset_(0),
          peak_offset_(0),
          owns_buffer_(false),
          is_locked_(false),
          space_(space) {}

    ~ArenaAllocator() override {
        if (owns_buffer_ && buffer_) {
            VirtualMemory::free(buffer_, capacity_, is_locked_);
            buffer_ = nullptr;
        }
    }

    ArenaAllocator(const ArenaAllocator&) = delete;
    ArenaAllocator& operator=(const ArenaAllocator&) = delete;

    [[nodiscard]] void* allocate(size_t bytes, size_t alignment = kDefaultAlignment) override {
        if (bytes == 0) return nullptr;
        if (alignment == 0 || (alignment & (alignment - 1)) != 0 || !buffer_) {
            throw std::bad_alloc();
        }
        const uintptr_t current_addr = reinterpret_cast<uintptr_t>(buffer_) + current_offset_;
        const size_t padding = (alignment - (current_addr & (alignment - 1))) & (alignment - 1);
        if (padding > capacity_ - current_offset_) {
            throw std::bad_alloc();
        }
        const size_t offset_after_padding = current_offset_ + padding;
        if (bytes > capacity_ - offset_after_padding) {
            throw std::bad_alloc();
        }
        current_offset_ = offset_after_padding + bytes;
        if (current_offset_ > peak_offset_) {
            peak_offset_ = current_offset_;
        }
        return reinterpret_cast<void*>(current_addr + padding);
    }

    void deallocate(void*, size_t, size_t) noexcept override {}

    [[nodiscard]] bool owns(const void* ptr) const noexcept override {
        if (!buffer_ || !ptr) return false;
        const auto p_addr = reinterpret_cast<uintptr_t>(ptr);
        const auto base_addr = reinterpret_cast<uintptr_t>(buffer_);
        return (p_addr >= base_addr) && (p_addr < (base_addr + capacity_));
    }

    [[nodiscard]] MemorySpace memory_space() const noexcept override { return space_; }

    void reset() noexcept override { current_offset_ = 0; }

    [[nodiscard]] size_t allocated_bytes() const noexcept { return current_offset_; }
    [[nodiscard]] size_t capacity() const noexcept { return capacity_; }
    [[nodiscard]] size_t available_bytes() const noexcept {
        return (current_offset_ < capacity_) ? (capacity_ - current_offset_) : 0;
    }
    [[nodiscard]] size_t peak_bytes() const noexcept { return peak_offset_; }
    [[nodiscard]] bool is_page_locked() const noexcept { return is_locked_; }
    [[nodiscard]] std::byte* data() noexcept { return buffer_; }
    [[nodiscard]] const std::byte* data() const noexcept { return buffer_; }

    class [[nodiscard]] ScopedMarker {
    public:
        explicit ScopedMarker(ArenaAllocator& arena) noexcept
            : arena_(arena), saved_offset_(arena.current_offset_) {}
        ~ScopedMarker() noexcept { arena_.current_offset_ = saved_offset_; }
        ScopedMarker(const ScopedMarker&) = delete;
        ScopedMarker& operator=(const ScopedMarker&) = delete;
    private:
        ArenaAllocator& arena_;
        size_t saved_offset_;
    };

    [[nodiscard]] ScopedMarker scope() noexcept { return ScopedMarker(*this); }

private:
    std::byte*  buffer_{nullptr};
    size_t      capacity_{0};
    size_t      current_offset_{0};
    size_t      peak_offset_{0};
    bool        owns_buffer_{false};
```
